# Marvin West Coast Distribution Center

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial)

Jan 21

Written By [Lisa Frisch](/portfolio-commercial?author=52715e4be4b0f3e367a30449)

### Client

Marvin

### Location

Reno, NV

### Size

113,400-sq-ft

### Designer

Tectonics Design Group

### Photos

Jeff Dow Photography and Motion

Marvin’s new 113,400-square-foot distribution center at Tolles Development’s Airway Commerce Center in Reno, Nevada, represents a pivotal step in Marvin’s strategy to enhance their West Coast distribution network. The facility not only reflects Marvin’s commitment to sustainability and operational efficiency but also showcases PB’s expertise in delivering high-quality construction tailored to Marvin’s operational needs and timeline.

**Meeting Marvin’s Operational Goals**

This facility was designed to consolidate Marvin’s operations, reduce transit times, and improve service to their dealer network. Key features, such as a custom racking system, a forklift management system, and an eco-conscious design with LED lighting and electric forklifts, ensure seamless logistics operations. Marvin’s focus on sustainability and innovation is evident throughout the space, which combines practical functionality with environmentally responsible choices.

**Realizing Marvin’s Vision**

PB collaborated with the Airway design team to meet Marvin’s ambitious timeline. PB oversaw the construction of essential systems, including IT infrastructure, mechanical systems, electrical systems, and sprinklers, as well as the fit-out of the facility’s office spaces, which include a reception/showroom, restrooms, private offices, a breakroom, and a conference room. By introducing a design-build electrician early in the process, PB expedited electrical design and installation, ensuring the project stayed on schedule.

**A Collaborative Process for Success**

PB’s ability to manage overlapping project schedules while maintaining high construction standards was critical to the project’s success. Working closely with Marvin and other stakeholders, PB ensured the facility was ready to support Marvin’s operational expansion from day one. Their proactive and precise approach underscores their role as a trusted partner for industrial projects.

**Built for Growth and Sustainability**

Marvin’s Reno distribution center is a milestone in their West Coast strategy, combining state-of-the-art logistics capabilities with sustainable practices. This collaborative project between Marvin and Plenium Builders highlights what is possible when innovation, precision, and shared goals align, ensuring both immediate operational benefits and long-term growth potential.

# Northern Nevada Public Health TB Clinic

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial)

Dec 19

Written By [Lisa Frisch](/portfolio-commercial?author=52715e4be4b0f3e367a30449)

### Client

Northern Nevada Public Health

### Location

Reno, NV

### Size

7,000-sq-ft

### Designer

H+K Architects

### Photos

Evan Petty Aerial Imaging

**A Vision of Safety and Hope Takes Shape**

For decades, Northern Nevada’s tuberculosis clinic worked tirelessly to serve the community, even as it operated out of a deteriorating 1950s-era facility originally built for an entirely different purpose. Patients arrived seeking care in a space never intended for modern infectious disease treatment, while staff worked to deliver critical services within the limits of an aging building.

That story has now changed. A new, purpose-built clinic stands on East 9th Street which was designed from the ground up to support safe, efficient tuberculosis care and strengthen public health in the region.

**A Facility for Public Health**

Northern Nevada Public Health set out to create a facility that could meet today’s standards for infection control, patient care, and operational efficiency. The completed ±7,500-square-foot building delivers on that vision, replacing the outdated Kirman Avenue clinic with a modern environment designed specifically for tuberculosis evaluation and treatment.

The new clinic includes negative air pressure exam rooms that protect patients and staff from airborne exposure, along with dedicated lab and specimen collection areas, welcoming waiting spaces, and functional offices and meeting rooms. Each element supports NNPH’s mission to identify, treat, and prevent the spread of tuberculosis across Washoe County.

**CMAR Leadership from Concept to Completion**

PB joined the project early as Construction Manager at Risk (CMAR), working alongside NNPH and the design team to guide the project from concept through completion. From the outset, PB provided site assessments, conceptual budgeting, and building systems evaluations to align the project’s goals with available funding and long-term operational needs.

Throughout design, PB led value engineering efforts, performed constructability reviews with BIM integration, and developed detailed cost models and schedules that accounted for long-lead materials and grant-driven timelines. Subcontractor prequalification and competitive bidding ensured the right trade partners were engaged early, while procurement strategies helped maintain momentum through critical phases of construction.

PB also coordinated phasing and logistics within the active West Hills campus, allowing the new building to be constructed efficiently within an existing site. The team worked closely with stakeholders to refine scope, support design decisions, and ultimately deliver a Guaranteed Maximum Price that balanced cost certainty with project quality.

**A Lasting Impact**

The new clinic supports smoother operations, enhanced infection control, and a more dignified experience for those receiving care. For PB, the project reflects the value of early collaboration and disciplined CMAR delivery.

# SCHEELS - Meridian, ID

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial)

Aug 3

Written By [Lisa Frisch](/portfolio-commercial?author=52715e4be4b0f3e367a30449)

### Client

SCHEELS

### Location

Meridian, ID

### Size

240,000-sq-ft

### Designer

RLE Group

### Awards

CoStar Group Impact Awards Commercial Development of the Year

The new SCHEELS All Sports Store in Meridian, Idaho, brings the iconic SCHEELS shopping experience to the Treasure Valley, combining retail with entertainment to create a one-of-a-kind destination. Plenium Builders partnered with SCHEELS on their 33rd location, a 240,000-square-foot flagship design that seamlessly blends specialty shopping with family-friendly attractions, all under one roof. From a 65-foot, 16-car Ferris wheel and a 16,000-gallon aquarium full of local aquatic species to interactive sports simulators and boutique shops, the store requires a mix of architectural finishes, fixtures, and features and a level of quality that represents the SCHEELS ethos of expertise, service, and selection.

_Aerial views of Scheels Meridian_

**Executing SCHEELS' Vision Through Careful Planning and Coordination**

Constructing a SCHEELS’ store is no small feat, and this project required a detailed, phased approach to ensure every element was delivered to SCHEELS’ high quality standards. The PB team coordinated phased turnovers, completing office and back-of-house areas six months before the grand opening. This approach allowed SCHEELS to move in and prepare for operations, all while our construction team continued work on the retail and entertainment spaces. By aligning with SCHEELS’ timeline and working proactively with local building and fire authorities, PB kept the project on track and minimized any potential disruptions to the store’s fit-out phase.

**Crafting a Comfortable Environment with Electrochromatic Glass**

A unique feature of the latest SCHEELS design by RLE Group is the use of electrochromatic glass, an innovative, electronically tintable material that automatically adjusts to sunlight levels. Unlike standard glass, electrochromatic glass provides dynamic shading, enhancing comfort and adding efficiency to energy management. Typically the manufacturer would have access to the finished openings to verify the sizes of the constructed openings. Due to the material’s long lead time, the glass was early in the project, before precise field measurements were available. To ensure a flawless installation, Plenium Builders implemented strict quality control measures, double- and triple- checking all rough openings to avoid installation issues.

**Problem-Solving for a Seamless Build**

Building a SCHEELS store comes with its own set of challenges, and our team faced each one head-on. When concrete supply issues arose, PB acted quickly, sourcing an alternative supplier to maintain the project’s pace. Similarly, two underperforming subcontractors were supplemented to ensure standards and timelines were met. Our team also tackled a delay in electrical gear procurement by devising temporary solutions to power essential areas, allowing construction to proceed without interruption. These proactive solutions reflect our commitment to ensuring that every phase of construction went smoothly.

**Bringing SCHEELS to Treasure Valley**

Plenium Builders takes pride in delivering a project that supports SCHEELS' vision for their flagship location in Idaho. Our focus on collaboration, careful planning, and quality assurance helped bring this 240,000-square-foot retail experience to life. From meticulous attention to detail to agile problem-solving, we approached every aspect of this build with SCHEELS’ goals in mind. SCHEELS an exacting and demanding client that is a pleasure to work with and we have enjoyed building this productive and positive project together.

# John McGraw and Silver Sage Apartments Renovations - Reno Housing Authority

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial)

Jul 3

Written By [Lisa Frisch](/portfolio-commercial?author=52715e4be4b0f3e367a30449)

### Client

Reno Housing Authority

### Location

Sparks, NV

### Size

23,500-sq-ft

### Designer

JK Architecture + Engineering

### Photos

Evan Petty Photography

**A Strategy Built Around Stability**

Reno Housing Authority set out to modernize this 14-building affordable living community to extend its usability. With waiting lists that can stretch up to two years from application to move-in, the complex represents a critical housing resource. Renovations required residents to temporarily leave their individual units, making RHA’s relocation planning essential to preserving stability within the community.

A CM-at-Risk delivery model allowed early collaboration with JK Architecture + Engineering and the broader design team to evaluate phasing, constructability, and cost alignment before work began.

The team organized the work into three clearly defined phases so improvements could progress while residents remained within the complex. RHA led temporary relocations with care, and the construction team sequenced activities around reliable timelines and clear communication to minimize disruption.

**Modernization with Purpose**

The team renovated kitchens, bathrooms, flooring, and finishes while improving accessibility throughout each unit. Trade partners upgraded mechanical, plumbing, and electrical systems to strengthen long-term performance and operational efficiency. Crews repaired the exterior envelope, implemented life safety improvements, and refreshed community and administrative spaces to better support resident programs and on-site services.

As construction progressed and crews opened walls and assemblies, the team identified original construction details that could limit the long-term effectiveness of the rehabilitation, including poorly constructed window frames, incompatible materials, and missing waterproofing components. The team brought these findings directly to the designer and Reno Housing Authority. Together, they evaluated cost-conscious solutions that protected the schedule while strengthening durability and performance.

That attention to detail ensured the improvements extended beyond surface updates and reinforced the long-term viability of the property.

**Coordinated Execution**

Occupied construction demanded more than sequencing trades. It required awareness that work unfolded around people’s homes. Crews coordinated daily with property management and consultants to plan activities, control noise, limit interruptions, and maintain safe pedestrian access throughout the site. Clear signage, predictable work hours, and courteous field conduct reduced distractions for residents navigating construction near their front doors.

Early CMAR engagement aligned the owner, designer, and builder before work began. The team used detailed scheduling and disciplined coordination to maintain reliable timelines. Project leaders communicated consistently with partners and residents as improvements progressed.

**Measured Results**

The team delivered the project ahead of schedule through early planning and steady coordination. Reno Housing Authority maintained housing stability while strengthening the community for long-term service. Collaboration between owner, designer, and builder reinforced the durability and performance of the improvements.

# Rosewood Nature Study Bridge

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial)

Nov 28

Written By [Lisa Frisch](/portfolio-commercial?author=52715e4be4b0f3e367a30449)

### Location

Reno, NV

### Size

1000-sq-ft

### Delivery

CM-at-Risk

### Designer

Truckee Meadows Parks Foundation

In September of 2023, Lauren Jilot, a Life Scout with Boy Scouts of America from BSA Troop 55 in Reno approached Plenium Builders (along with her mom) with a request for help improving her community. She had uncovered an urgent need to repair the bridge at the Old Rosewood Golf Course. Its aging deck surface—repaired multiple times with plywood—was impeding full-sized vehicles (including emergency vehicles) from crossing and limiting access to the Truckee Meadows Parks Foundation (TMPF) Rosewood Nature Study trail. She made it her Eagle Project, a leadership-based Scout activity to help TMPF fund and manage the needed repairs.

Lauren's five-page project brief included sketches, a proposed schedule, and material selections for the 100-foot-long, 10-foot-wide bridge. She investigated permit requirements and got the permission she needed from local authorities and TMPF. Thinking ahead about logistics, she snipped views from Google Earth and reviewed the paths of travel. She engaged her own team of volunteers to clear the road of debris and brush to provide construction access. Lauren held fundraisers and arranged donations for the bridge repair project.

PB's estimator worked with Tahoe Truckee Lumber to develop a material estimate. After learning more about the leadership on Lauren’s part and the benefit to the community, TTL provided the new material at ½ cost. Plenium Builders donated our carpenters' time to complete the work. They re-decked the bridge, replacing straps, ties, screws, and bolts. As a bonus, the repairs add access to overflow event parking and storage, and make it easier to maintain the trails.

The team was excited about the result of this Scout’s efforts, an updated and useful bridge with clear access. A small contribution, but a big project for us. Thank you, Lauren, for the exceptional project management and for your commitment to improving our community!

# Incline High School Expansion

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial)

Sep 21

Written By [Lisa Frisch](/portfolio-commercial?author=52715e4be4b0f3e367a30449)

### Client

Washoe County School District

### Location

Incline Village, NV

### Size

116,000-sq-ft

### Designer

Collaborative Design Studio

### Photos

In1View Media

**Bringing Big Ideas to a Mountain Campus**

High-elevation construction always requires foresight—but even more so when the site is an occupied school above Lake Tahoe. Between weather, environmental restrictions, and regional access challenges, delivering this multi-phase project demanded proactive planning, tight coordination, and extensive knowledge of how to build in the basin.

**A Centerpiece for Career Pathways**

The heart of the expansion is a robust culinary arts program. Students now gain direct career experience in a fully outfitted commercial training kitchen, instructional studio, and student-run café. Every element was constructed with durability, code compliance, and flexibility in mind, ensuring the spaces serve both educational needs and real-world workflows.

**Smart Upgrades, Seamlessly Delivered**

Other improvements included new facilities for the ROTC program, modernized administrative space, and select classroom renovations. A new student hub capitalizes on the building’s mountain setting, offering daylight-filled areas for group study, collaboration, and downtime. Interior renovations were timed carefully around the school’s calendar to minimize disruption.

**Safe, Secure, and On Schedule**

Security upgrades across the campus were another critical component. Installation of new systems was completed after hours, with advance coordination and logistical staging to avoid impacts to students or staff. In a live school environment, sequencing and communication were key to making progress while maintaining day-to-day operations.

**Local Expertise Matters**

Remote access, limited subcontractor availability, and snow-prone schedules challenged nearly every phase of construction. The team drew on deep regional experience to overcome permitting hurdles, maintain efficient deliveries, and comply with Lake Tahoe’s strict water quality rules. Coordination with district leaders and on-site staff ensured real-time adjustments kept the work moving forward.

**A Foundation for Future Generations**

Careful phasing, attention to detail, and steady field leadership helped bring this campus transformation to life. The result is a high-functioning, student-focused learning environment designed to meet today’s needs and built to last through tomorrow’s.

[View fullsize](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/6234ed17ed89810e30ea47f5/1a0c4788-0711-457f-8edd-dc311e20c489/ihs-lake-tahoe-incline-village.jpg) 

[View fullsize](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/6234ed17ed89810e30ea47f5/4045bd49-2233-4aa2-993e-ba8a4caa4c3c/incline-high-school-expansion.jpg)

# COMMERCIAL Portfolio

[![Ramsay’s Kitchen and Gordon Ramsay Fish &amp; Chips](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/6234ed17ed89810e30ea47f5/a93e3dbe-f1d3-4c8b-b6f5-925a8968e44b/ramsays-kitchen-at-the-row-reno-nv_3.jpg)](/portfolio-commercial/ramsays-kitchen-fish-and-chips-reno)

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial) Lisa Frisch 9/8/23 [Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial) Lisa Frisch 9/8/23

# [Ramsay’s Kitchen and Gordon Ramsay Fish & Chips](/portfolio-commercial/ramsays-kitchen-fish-and-chips-reno)

Two new Gordon Ramsay restaurants—Ramsay’s Kitchen and Gordon Ramsay Fish & Chips—now energize The ROW in Reno. Plenium Builders tackled complex construction challenges, delivering elegant custom finishes, an open-concept bar, and British-themed designs. Built within an active casino, these vibrant spaces elevate Reno’s dining scene with world-class craftsmanship and immersive ambiance.

[Read More](/portfolio-commercial/ramsays-kitchen-fish-and-chips-reno)

[![Dick Scott Manor - Reno Housing Authority](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/6234ed17ed89810e30ea47f5/249ddb6b-ab84-4de6-9cd3-afa9cb8e228c/dick-scott-manor-RHA-vets-supportive-housing_med-4.jpg)](/portfolio-commercial/dick-scott-manor)

[Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial) Lisa Frisch 9/6/23 [Commercial](/portfolio-commercial/category/Commercial) Lisa Frisch 9/6/23

# [Dick Scott Manor - Reno Housing Authority](/portfolio-commercial/dick-scott-manor)

Dick Scott Manor is a 12-unit permanent supportive housing project for veterans at risk of homelessness. Funded by ARPA and foundation grants, and owned by the Reno Housing Authority, it was delivered by Plenium Builders with full funding compliance, constructability input, and expert coordination on a tight urban infill site.

[Read More](/portfolio-commercial/dick-scott-manor)

# [Horiba Instruments R&D Facility](/portfolio-commercial/horiba-reno-research-development-facility)

HORIBA’s Nevada facility has evolved through a long-term design-build partnership. Over multiple phases, the team delivered cleanrooms, labs, mezzanines, and utility systems supporting the development of precision instruments for semiconductor and scientific use—completed with close coordination, technical rigor, and minimal disruption to ongoing operations.

[Read More](/portfolio-commercial/horiba-reno-research-development-facility)

# [City of Reno Public Safety Center](/portfolio-commercial/reno-public-safety-center)

An award-winning adaptive reuse, this project transformed a newspaper plant into Reno’s public safety headquarters with major demolition, seismic upgrades, and custom systems for police operations. Sustainable features and close collaboration shaped a facility ready for emergencies and the City’s evolving needs—demonstrating how smart teamwork can strengthen community resilience.

[Read More](/portfolio-commercial/reno-public-safety-center)

# [City of Reno Development Services Department Offices](/portfolio-commercial/reno-development-department-offices)

Plenium Builders remodeled a City Hall office for Reno’s Development Department, creating a brighter, more functional workspace. Updated finishes, flexible layouts, and collaborative meeting areas support both staff workflow and public service. The design reflects the Department’s role in guiding projects that shape Reno’s built environment.

# [Western Nevada Supply Distribution Center](/portfolio-commercial/western-nevada-supply-warehouse)

Western Nevada Supply expanded its Sparks campus with a 74,500-square-foot warehouse and remodel to enhance fulfillment capacity and operational flow. The facility joins a series of campus improvements completed over years of partnership, with additional design-build expansions extending the company’s infrastructure for evolving logistics, storage, and customer service needs.

# [Western Nevada Supply Offices](/portfolio-commercial/western-nevada-supply-offices)

A remodel of the Blue Team’s two-story building created a modern, connected workspace linking sales, showroom, and service areas. Staged construction kept business uninterrupted. Features include custom millwork, barn door–style whiteboards, built-in ladders, and integrated AV systems, all supporting the team’s collaborative culture and reflecting current industry trends.

[Read More](/portfolio-commercial/western-nevada-supply-offices)
